‘Ramayana,’ ‘Fatherland,’ ‘Dhurandhar’ Lead International Film Festival of India Lineup Featuring Almodovar, Kore-eda, Kurosawa

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Paweł Pawlikowski’s “Fatherland,” which earned him the best director prize at Cannes this year, will open the 57th International Film Festival of India (IFFI), set for Nov. 20-28 in Goa.

Nitesh Tiwari’s “Ramayana: Rise of a Legend” will open the festival’s BRICS showcase, and Aditya Dhar’s Bollywood feature “Dhurandhar” will open the Indian Panorama.

Goa chief minister Pramod Sawant unveiled the program at a curtain-raiser press conference in the state, joined by festival director Ashutosh Gowariker, the filmmaker behind “Lagaan.” Organizers received more than 1,900 submissions from 137 countries for this edition, and the lineup spans more than 150 international films from 86 countries. Related Stories Gkids Acquires U.S. Rights to Cartoon Saloon’s Animated Film 'Julián,' Directed by Louise Bagnall and Executive Produced by Zoe Saldaña (EXCLUSIVE) Arnold Schwarzenegger Explains to Jimmy Kimmel Why He’s ‘Absolutely’ Still a Republican After the Blue ‘Tsunami’ Midterms

“Fatherland” is set in 1949. It tracks Nobel laureate Thomas Mann and his daughter Erika as they travel through a Germany split by the aftermath of war. The Poland, Germany, Italy and France co-production plays in German, English, French and Russian.

Alina Șerban’s “I Matter,” a Romania, Germany and Belgium co-production in Romanian and Romani, will screen as the mid-fest film. Anthony Chen’s Singaporean comedy-drama “We Are All Strangers,” in Mandarin and English, will close the festival.

Chanchal Kumar, secretary of India’s Ministry of Information & Broadcasting, quoted Prime Minister Narendra Modi in discussing this year’s theme, “Stories Without Borders”: “The stories may have an address, but emotions have no borders.”

The BRICS section will feature work from all 11 member nations of the bloc as well as its partner countries. Titles include Jessica Beshir’s “Faya Dayi” and Anastasia Kuzina’s “Bo Bo.”

Spain will bring four films as country of honor: Pedro Almodóvar’s “Bitter Christmas,” Jaime Lorente’s “The Bad Son,” Laura Alvea and José Ortuño’s “Trinidad” and Juana Macías’ “Looking for Michael.” Russia’s partner-country showcase pairs Kuzina’s “Bo Bo” with Nikita Grushin’s “New Home,” Raul Geidarov’s “Sidney Lumet’s Birthday” and Gerbert Rappaport’s musical “Cheryomushki.”

The festival will screen 76 Indian films across 18 languages. Indian Panorama selectors weighed 404 feature and 358 non-feature submissions, while the best web series (OTT) category attracted 46 eligible entries. Leena Yadav’s Ladakhi-language “Becoming” will open the non-feature strand.

Madhur Bhandarkar chairs the feature film jury and Himanshu Shekhar Khatua heads the non-feature film jury.

Indian director-producer Aanand L. Rai (“Raanjhanaa,” “Tanu Weds Manu”) will chair the international competition jury. He will be joined by Argentine filmmaker Sol Berruezo Pichon-Riviere; Thai writer-director Ratchapoom Boonbunchachoke, whose “A Useful Ghost” won the grand prize at Cannes Critics’ Week in 2025; Colombian sound designer María Alejandra Rojas; and Romanian writer-director Bogdan Mureșanu (“The New Year That Never Came”).

The Cinema of the World section includes Kore-eda Hirokazu’s “Look Back,” Kurosawa Kiyoshi’s “The Samurai and the Prisoner,” Lukas Dhont’s “Coward,” Nishikawa Miwa’s “Children Untold,” Gábor Reisz’s “Ground Floor” and Wim Wenders’ “From Inside Out – The Architecture of Peter Zumthor.” From Festivals Worldwide gathers Hong Sang-soo’s “Nowhere to Lay My Eyes,” Emin Alper’s “Salvation,” Valeska Grisebach’s “The Dreamed Adventure” and Grant Gee’s “Everybody Digs Bill Evans.”

Two sections are new this year, with five films each. Animation includes Brad Bird’s “Ray Gunn.” Films About Films was created after leading filmmakers suggested it. Its titles include Barnaby Thompson’s “Maverick: The Epic Adventures of David Lean” and Francesco Zippel’s “Vittorio De Sica – Staging Life.”

Seven first features will compete for the best debut feature prize: “Fading Man” (Germany), “The Sledge” (India), “Paris” (Belgium), “The Black Sea” (Egypt), “L’Âge d’Or (The Golden Age)” (France and Italy), “Birds on Ice” (India) and “Atlantic” (China). The wider program counts 52 women directors and 62 first-time filmmakers.

Restored Classics will screen Don Siegel’s “Invasion of the Body Snatchers,” Danny Boyle’s “Trainspotting,” David Lynch’s “Wild at Heart,” F.W. Murnau’s “Sunrise: A Song of Two Humans” and Ernst Lubitsch’s “To Be or Not to Be.” Andrzej Wajda gets a five-film centenary tribute. Indian figures born a century ago will also be honored: directors Shakti Samanta and Raj Khosla, actors Uttam Kumar, Suryakant Mandhare, Prem Nazir and Raj Kumar, and composers O.P. Nayyar and Ravi.

The WAVES Film Bazaar market will host 150 invited guests, including buyers and production companies. Its program covers a Screenwriters’ Lab, a Co-production Market, a Viewing Room and a Work-in-Progress Lab. Netflix is the bazaar’s Knowledge Series partner and will present the gala premiere of “Pushpak Viman,” alongside an international screening of “Ray Gunn.” The streamer will also highlight its work with the Indian government on building the country’s VFX talent base.

Screenings will take place on 13 indoor screens at four locations: Panaji, Porvorim, Margao and Ponda. Together they seat more than 4,000.

IFFI was launched in 1952 and ranks among Asia’s oldest film festivals. It is organized by the information and broadcasting ministry with the Goa government and the Entertainment Society of Goa.

4 Hours Saved, 20 Hours In The Air: Will Passengers Choose Qantas’ Project Sunrise?

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Well over 20 hours on a single flight sounds like hell to a lot of passengers. But enduring this will be necessary for those wishing to use Qantas Project Sunrise flights, which are set to begin in 2027 with services from Sydney Kingsford-Smith Airport (SYD) to London Heathrow Airport (LHR).

Qantas will also demand a premium, arguing that passengers can expect to save around four hours and avoid the hassle of an international transfer. Despite the clear difficulties that Project Sunrise could pose, particularly for economy class passengers, Qantas is banking on its success.

Airways Magazine reports that the airline expects to add AU$400 million ($274 million) in earnings before interest and tax through the services. High demand will be vital to secure such positive results, particularly given that challenges such as high fuel costs, a weakened Australian dollar and higher labor and airport costs have emerged since 2022.

Project Sunrise's opening route will be between SYD and LHR, a mammoth route of approximately 9,188 nautical miles (17,000 km). Traveling from Britain to Australia currently usually requires a change, which is often in Singapore or the Middle East. Such stopovers make the route longer, and passengers also have to waste time on the ground.

It is expected that Project Sunrise flights will take around 21 hours and 40 minutes. This will save approximately four hours. In return for the time savings and the unique nature of this flight, Qantas expects to receive a 20% higher Revenue per Available Seat Kilometer (RASK) compared with comparable one-stop itineraries.

When it arrives in 2027, Project Sunrise will overtake Singapore Airlines Airbus A350-900ULR flights as the world's longest. That will secure Qantas' position as the leading premium airline in Australia and a key player among the airlines that offer ultra-long-haul flying. However, regardless of the added convenience, some passengers will struggle with being on an aircraft for over 20 hours, while others will find the higher price off-putting.

41% of seats will be in premium cabins, versus 30% in premium cabins for Qantas' Boeing 787-9 and Airbus A380, Airways Mag reports. These seats are divided between first, business, and premium economy class. Such a high number of premium seats takes inspiration from Singapore Airlines' A350-900ULRs, which are exclusively fitted with premium economy and business class seats.

It's not surprising that Qantas expects these products to be popular, given that a flight of over 20 hours will give passengers the opportunity to take advantage of the luxury. The table below uses Qantas' data to show the key specifications for the A350-1000ULR. It covers both the aircraft itself and the four cabins onboard.

9,700-10,000 nautical miles (18,000-18,520 km)

The ultimate in luxury comes from first class, a product that Singapore Airlines has skipped on its A350-900ULR. These six suites are distinguished from business class by a separate armchair and a true flat bed, rather than one that reclines. This class also has a huge entertainment screen, along with a flexible space for eating and working with up to two guests.

Behind first class is an enormous 52-seat business class cabin. The suite has a privacy door and totals 42 inches (100 cm) wide. At 80 inches (2 m), the flat bed is one inch (2.5 cm) longer than what Qantas offers on its Airbus A380 business class product. All but the very tallest of guests should find this roomy enough. Finally, customers can choose from within a 40-seat premium economy cabin.

These seats are in a 2-4-2 configuration and have 40 inches (100 cm) of pitch. Australian designer David Caon says that “the Premium Economy cabin has been redesigned from the ground up with a focus on ergonomics, entertainment and privacy.” The new headrest wings are the biggest visible difference and will provide passengers with both additional support and a sense of privacy without isolating them from travel companions.

The main reason that Qantas could fail is an inability to sell economy class seats. Many passengers on a budget would rather take the hit of a longer total travel time for the chance to stretch their legs during a one-stop itinerary. Before the ongoing crisis in the Middle East, it was also popular to use the stop as an excuse to enjoy a short vacation in the region, such as in Dubai, before the main trip to Australia or the United Kingdom.

Qantas hopes it can sway wary passengers through a high-quality economy class product. The cabin has 140 seats, with 42 in Economy Plus. These seats are in a 3-3-3 configuration. A relatively generous 33 inches (83.8 cm) of pitch was initially promised, although Qantas has now backtracked to saying the pitch will vary in different parts of the aircraft.

22 inches (56 cm) in the separate reclining armchair

A six-way adjustable headrest gives a greater degree of customization compared to what you can usually expect in economy class. Two fast-charging outlets per seat also means that your entertainment won't run out. Economy Plus will be broadly the same but with priority boarding and access to overhead lockers, as well as a little bit more legroom. The table above uses Qantas' data to compare the seat dimensions that you can enjoy, or suffer in, across each class.

However, initial predictions are that Qantas will attract strong demand for its economy class seats aboard Project Sunrise, partly because it has intentionally produced a small economy class cabin. Executive Traveller's case study predicts a load factor of 85%. This is lower than what is expected in the premium classes, but still more than enough to sustain the service.

Regardless of the quality of the economy class, you would still be spending over 20 hours in a seat designed for affordability rather than comfort. Some airlines that fly ultra-long-haul routes are getting creative, such as United Airlines' Relax Row, which makes lying flat in economy class possible.

The Australian carrier's answer to make such a long time in economy class possible is its Wellbeing Zone, which is available to both economy and premium economy class passengers. Qantas calls it "an on board destination to promote movement, stretching and hydration throughout your flight."

Within the Wellbeing Zone, you'll find a selection of beverages and refreshments available through self-service. The area will also have monitors to guide you through movements, ensuring you can stay mobile during the flight. "Sculpted surfaces and integrated handles" will also facilitate stretching. Qantas Group Chief Executive Officer Alan Joyce said that:

“Fewer seats translate to more space for each customer and a dedicated Wellbeing Zone for travellers to stretch, help themselves to a snack and spend time out of their seat. We are the only airline in the world that will have a bespoke designed onboard stretch and movement space,".

Airbus' A350-1000ULR begins testing as Project Sunrise moves closer to reality.

Qantas has made a vast investment to order 12 bespoke A350-1000ULR aircraft that will carry out the services. However, it is also investing time and personnel to get Project Sunrise off the ground. It will train over 360 pilots and 1,200 cabin crew, or 30 pilots and 100 cabin crew per aircraft, in the unique skills required for these services.

Such a substantial training operation is needed because of regulatory arrangements Qantas has made with the Civil Aviation Safety Authority, requiring far more crew members aboard every Project Sunrise flight. The Project Sunrise pilot training is occurring at a simulator in Sydney.

It is Australia's first A350 simulator, which was installed ahead of the first A350-1000ULR's arrival. The first cohort has been drawn from Qantas' current A330 pilots, with 40 involved in the first wave. That decision was made because of numerous commonalities between the A350 and the A330.

It's a race against time to get the crews ready for the expected first A350-1000ULR delivery in April 2027. It is currently expected that this first aircraft will fly to New Zealand to take training out of the simulator and familiarize the crew with its operations. The jet will then be pressed into regular service to the United Kingdom and, later, the US.

Jamie Dornan & Anthony Mackie Share Hilarious Lesson From Fatherhood

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Jamie Dornan and Anthony Mackie—who are costarring in the thriller series 12 12 12—exclusively told E! News about their kids' reactions to growing up with movie star dads.

Jamie Dornan and Anthony Mackie's most important critics are their kids.

The actors—who first starred in 2019's Synchronic before reuniting for upcoming TV series 12 12 12—confessed that amid their many roles as action stars over the years, earning cool points from their children has been no easy feat.

"If they don't think I'm cool after Lord of the Rings, I'm done," Dornan, who plays Aragorn in the upcoming prequel, The Hunt for Gollum, joked in an exclusive interview with E! News’ Will Marfuggi. "If that's not good enough for my kids, then I don't know what else to offer them."

The 50 Shades of Grey alum brought his three kids Dulcie, 13, Elva, 10 and Alberta, 7, with wife Amelia Warner to stay with him in New Zealand during the fantasy shoot, where they were thankfully impressed by his performance on set.

"Usually, they ask to leave. Like, they come to set, and after half an hour, they're like, 'You're just saying the same thing over and over. This is so boring,'" Dornan admitted. "They came to set one night when I was fighting and beheading orcs and stuff, and they were like, 'It was cool.'"

That said, the 44-year-old was pleased that his little ones found it "good to see Daddy all heroic like that" because, as he put it, "that'll be as cool as it gets for me."

Mackie's brood, on the other hand—which includes four sons with ex-wife Sheletta Chapital—weren't so easily won over by their dad moonlighting as a soldier with supernatural powers, but for good reason. The Captain America actor is already a superhero at home.

"My boys could care less," Mackie remarked with a laugh. "At the end of the day, I realized they just want me to be dad."

And the 48-year-old understands why his family unit is more content with quality time at home, including playing pickleball.

"I love the fact that all they want from me is me," he continued. "But then when I come home, we get to be one big family."

Still, Mackie admitted bringing the crew as his plus-fours to a red carpet was more than a typical 'bring your kids to work' day, even for them.

"They went to the premiere of my movie," Mackie recounted. "They had never seen anything of that scale, like of that magnitude."

And Mackie and Dornan's latest project is yet another grand production. The Marvel alum credited the art department when remarking, "The scale of the show, I feel, is kind of like another character."

The Belfast native agreed the ambitious approach paid off for the series, premiering Nov. 13 on Apple TV, about a meticulously planned fictional robbery of a bank in Zurich.

"It's like the biggest heist in European history," Dornan said, using the British slang for cheap when adding, "It couldn't be pony. It needed to be big."

New episodes drop on Fridays through Dec. 25.

Man City have 'certain' Gianluigi Donnarumma exit route, hurdle must be overcome - Expert

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Manchester City could potentially offload struggling goalkeeper Gianluigi Donnarumma to the Saudi Pro League next summer.

That is according to former Everton chief executive Keith Wyness, speaking exclusively to Football Insider, who believes that the Citizens' options are limited in terms of where they can move Donnarumma.

The 27-year-old has faced scrutiny for his performances of late, with his current 65.8 per cent save percentage standing him 13th in the ranking of Premier League starting goalkeepers.

Wyness believes that the Saudi Pro League could provide a genuine option for City to cash in on the Italian, whose wages are likely to pose an issue for several European clubs.

Donnarumma was widely regarded as one of the world's leading shot-stoppers when he arrived at the Etihad Stadium back in 2025, but is seeing his reputation damaged a little of late.

The former PSG ace is on a significant contract with the Citizens, but is not producing the goods for them at the moment.

Everton’s former chief Wyness – who served as CEO at Goodison Park between 2004 and 2009 and now runs a football consultancy advising elite clubs – believes that a move to Saudi Arabia could be an option for Donnarumma, but is clear that City would have to play the market carefully.

The Pro League recently introduced a new budget cap which prevents clubs from spending over 75 per cent of their revenue on their first-team squad.

Speaking on the latest edition of Football Insider's Inside Track podcast, Wyness explained how that has created a hurdle for City to overcome with Donnarumma.

"Yeah, I do (think he could be sold). I mean, if this would have been just a year ago, I'd have said the Saudi market would probably have taken him up," he said.

"But, you know, the budget restrictions may be starting to bite down there slightly. So it's not quite the option for clubs that it once was.

"Although, in Donnarumma's case, I still think it could be one option, certainly."

Former Man City financial advisor Stefan Borson recently told Football Insider that Donnarumma is becoming a "very expensive problem" for his former club.

The football finance expert believes that Enzo Maresca and his staff simply cannot continue to accept the gloveman's mistakes longt-

Borson believes that a return to Italy is the clear leading option for Donnarumma in terms of an escape route, but pointed out that he would likely need to take a sizeable pay cut.

The shot-stopper is becoming a big issue for the Citizens, especially as their 114 financial charges continue to loom over them.

#PostPulpit mailbag: What’s going on with Christian Gonzalez?

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The New England Patriots have landed in Germany where they hold a pair of practices before taking on the Detroit Lions at 9:30 a.m. ET on Sunday.

So, let's get into this week's edition of the #PostPulpit Mailbag.

Like a lot of people, I was mightily disappointed (to say the least) in the recent performance of our resident highest-paid cornerback in the league. But I find myself wondering if he's the only one deserving blame. When he repeatedly lines up in such soft coverage, not pressing at the line of scrimmage — indeed, giving the receiver an obscenely huge cushion to release — that's not entirely HIS decision, right? Isn't that pre-snap alignment dictated by the coaches and their game plan, at least much of the time? How much of the problem do you think lies with coaching schemes that play away from Gonzalez's strengths? – BriGuy301

What happened to Gonzalez last game and why? Was it a one-off, or did the Packers figure something out that other teams might be able to emulate? – fdsafdsa

Gonzalez is certainly not blameless and needs to play better. However, he is also being asked to do a lot within a Patriots secondary that leans heavily on disguising coverages. While the All-Pro cornerback is at his best pressing receivers at the line of scrimmage and playing man coverage, he has been asked to handle a variety of responsibilities, including off-man coverage, inverted Cover 2 (where he is responsible for the half-field safety), playing the deep third in Cover 3 and more.

The question here is whether the Patriots have leaned too heavily into those disguises and reached a point where simplifying things would benefit the defense. New England has a talented secondary that is playing well below its capabilities as they rank dead last in the league in zone coverage EPA per dropback. Opposing offenses like the Bears and Packers have also appeared to be one step ahead with coverage beaters and using tools like quick snaps to catch the defense out of position and generate explosive plays.

At some point, the Patriots may need to prioritize letting their defensive backs play more straight up and to their strengths.

With all the pressure that Maye faces each game, and especially against teams like the Packers who blitz a lot (46.5% of his drop backs against Green Bay), why do we not see more hot reads or short patterns? It looks like the Patriots rely predominantly on mid- to long-range routes, which take more time and require the Offensive Line to hold up longer. I would think that Pop or Chism – who both have short-area quickness – would be great at those routes, especially given that they seem to run a lot of them during the preseason. So I'm curious if A) they're doing it but I just don't see it? And/or B) is there a good reason to not run quick slants and crossers and hot reads? – PatsFanFromNH

The Patriots' offense does feature sight adjustments and hot routes. Earlier this season, players credited opposing defenses for dropping defenders off the line of scrimmage to take away those routes. New England will need to find ways to counter those adjustments or develop other answers against the pressure packages that have repeatedly given the offense trouble.

In recent weeks, both the Bears and Packers have found success attacking the Patriots' offensive line with overload pressures, sending two defenders off the same side of the formation and dropping defenders off the line of scrimmage on the other side. In both games, New England struggled to pick up those pressures early before settling down and finding answers as the game progressed.

Moving forward, the Patriots need to determine how they want to handle those situations out of the gate, whether that means identifying the right protection calls before the snap or trusting Maye and his receivers to make plays after. Finding those answers earlier in games will be critical to avoid early deficits.

Brian, if the Patriots lose to the Lions to fall to 5-5 with 4 very difficult games left, is it time to switch gears and root for draft position? It's been a tough year full of injuries. That happens in football, and the draft is supposed to be a good one. On the other hand, if they pull off the upset in Germany, it's full speed ahead going for the playoffs, with a bye week plus the Chargers game to get healthy. – New England Guy

Sunday's game against the Lions feels like an inflection point on the season, so it will be interesting to see which Patriots team shows up in Germany.

As for the playoffs, 10 wins has historically been a good benchmark for securing a postseason spot. If the Patriots take care of business against the winless Chargers, Jets and Dolphins, they would need just two more wins from their remaining matchups against the Lions, Vikings, Chiefs, Bills and Broncos to reach that mark.

And looking at the current AFC landscape, the threshold could be closer to nine wins. There appears to be a relatively clear-cut top seven in the conference unless the Houston Texans catch fire during the second half of the season.

Why didn't the pats trade for a nose tackle, 3rd safety, or/and a 3rd outside cornerback? Any one, two or all 3 of these positions were a must have to right the ship! – sjamesbossports

After investing significant draft capital in A.J. Brown and looking to avoid taking on a major financial commitment, the Patriots were never likely to make a significant addition at the trade deadline. You could certainly make the argument for adding defensive depth in exchange for late-round draft capital, but the lack of movement at those positions around the league suggests there wasn't much of a market at those spots.

A question more long term :what are your thoughts about next year patriots cap big moves renegotiation with major players (or potential cut or trade) to absorb anticipated Maye super contract ? – 7i165

Who do you think will be the cap casualties next year? Onwenu, Barmore, Davis, Hunter, Byard, DeVitto, Moses, Landry? I know they are a veteran core, but almost all of them don't produce to what they are being paid at, so we might need to cut bait. How much would we gain from trading or letting them walk? – Maxxwell

The Patriots did not sell at the deadline as they still can be a competitve team especially as they get healthier on offense. It will be a different conversation this offseason as there is definitely an argument for the team getting younger and adding draft capital.

Among the names mentioned, I would imagine the team moves on from Christian Barmore, Harold Landry, and potentially Carlton Davis as the veterans have no guaranteed money remaining. Byard's play has not been worthy of a second contract while the guess would be Moses retires. Henry was extended this offseason while there's no rush to move off DeVito. All the moves would free up a substantial amount of salary cap space.

That's all for this week's #PostPulpit mailbag. If you have questions you'd liked to be answered next week, submit them online in our weekly submission post on the Feed. Make sure to follow @iambrianhines and @PatsPulpit as well.

Lions vs. Patriots preview, prediction: On Paper

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The Detroit Lions and New England Patriots are in Germany for a big international matchup. The Patriots are a bit desperate for a win, sitting at 5-4 and clinging to the final playoff spot in the AFC. The Lions are a little more comfortable in the fifth spot of the NFC playoff race, but if they want to contend in a competitive NFC North, they need this game, too.

So which team has that statistical advantage and will come out on top Sunday morning? Let's dive into the numbers with our Week 10 Lions vs. Patriots On Paper preview and prediction.

Note: Stats may be a little lighter this week because one of my bigger resources—NFL Pro—is not available in Germany.

The Detroit Lions had their worst passing offense performance last week against a bad—but improving—Dolphins defense. Offensive line struggles were the lowlight of the performance, headlined by the "suspension" of left guard Christian Mahogany and inconsistent play of rookie right tackle Blake Miller. Mahogany is back in the lineup, and that should help the Lions' passing offense which has remained near a top-five unit for most of the season. They rank:

And despite the poor offensive performance, the Lions offensive line is still grading out well as a unit. Though they rank 17th in sack percentage (6.5), they're fifth in pressure percentage allowed (19.4%), second in PFF pass blocking grade (75.1), and seventh in pass block win rate (54%).

The Patriots pass defense started off strong, but has struggled as of late, even against some of the most medicore-to-bad passing offenses. For the season, they rank:

For comparison's sake, all of these rankings except for dropback EPA are below the Lions' pass defense. So whatever you think of Detroit's ability to defend the pass, the Patriots have been worse.

But much like Detroit's pass defense, the one place they thrive is pressuring the opposing quarterback. Thought they're just 19th in pressure percentage (22.4), they're sixth in sack rate (8.0%), 11th in pass rush win rate (52%), and second in PFF pass rush grade (82.8).

While EDGE Elijah Ponder leads the team with 7.5 sacks, it's really New England's interior pressure that causes havoc. Look out for Christian Barmore (78.1 PFF pass rush grade) and Milton Williams (20 pressures) to give Detroit's interior offensive line some issues.

But New England's second is very beatable. Christian Gonzalez is having a down year (50.4 PFF grade) and the Patriots will be down Carlton Davis.

Player to watch: Amon-Ra St. Brown. This isn't just a Germany thing, St. Brown should thrive against the Patriots' zone-heavy defense. Brown is the Lions' best weapon when it comes to recognizing the soft spot in zones, just like last week's eight-catch, 86-yard performance.

Advantage: Lions +2.5. The Patriots' pass rush does have a chance to disrupt Jared Goff like the Dolphins did last week, but for most of the day, the Lions quarterback should be picking apart this poor secondary.

The inconsistency of this rushing attack is still very frustrating to watch, but putting things into perspective, it's still a very decent run game in Detroit. For the season, they rank:

Negative plays remain a huge problem, with Detroit's 20% stuffed rate ranking 25th in the NFL. They are improving in short-yardage situations, though, climbing to 18th in power success rate.

Still, the offensive line—though still plagued by some mental mistakes—appear to be moving people. Detroit ranks fourth in adjusted line yards, eighth in run block win rate, and, as a whole, the Lions entire team is fourth in PFF's run blocking grade.

Detroit will face a formidable test this week against a run defense that has allowed 100 rushing yards just three times this entire season, and each of their last five opponents have been held at or below 4.0 yards per carry. Though last week's performance against the Packers' worst rushing attack in the league should provide at least some hope.

Interestingly, the Patriots run defense appears more efficient than the sum of their parts. They rank just 16th in adjusted line yards allowed, 16th in run stop win rate, and 21st in PFF run defense grade.

One thing they do thrive in, however, is limiting explosives. They've only allowed six rushing plays of 15+ yards (third fewest) and just a single run of 20+ yards (t-fewest). But they're just okay in short-yardage situations. They're 10th in power success rate allowed (59%), but sport just a 16% stuffed rate (23rd).

Player to watch: LB Christian Ellis. While the Patriots are not particularly strong up the middle in run defense, Ellis sports the eighth-best PFF grade against the run (82.3), providing a strong second level of defense. The Patriots' secondary is also active and strong against the run.

Advantage: Lions +0.5. Detroit hasn't been as explosive as expected running the football this year, but they have found some consistency running for gains of 5-10 yards, and that could be exactly what we see on Sunday.

Despite a horrible start to the year, Drake Maye has seemingly righted the ship—outside of last week's performance against a very solid Packers pass defense. For the entire year, the Patriots pass offense ranks:

Essentially, if the Patriots could stop turning the ball over, they'd be a top-five passing attack. But turnovers have been a serious problem. Maye's 11 interceptions leads the league and his 4.0% interception percentage is third-highest in the NFL. To sport that dishonor and still produce the 10th-highest dropback EPA is pretty crazy.

So what's causing the high interception rate? It's a little bit of everything. An injured offensive line that ranks 21st in pressure rate (23.6%) and 18th in sack rate (6.6%), a quarterback who is still young and figuring things out, and a receiving corps that lost Romeo Doubs for the season and hasn't had a lot of A.J. Brown available.

But, again, the Patriots passing attack remains pretty dang efficient despite those problems.

I'm still hesitant to believe in Detroit's defensive improvement through the air. Much of that appears based on simply playing a bunch of poor passing attacks, but the one aspect they have seriously improved on is explosive plays allowed. In the first four games, the Lions allowed 20 passing plays of 20 yards (most in NFL). In the last four games, the Lions have allowed 14 (still t-10th most). For the season, Detroit still only ranks:

The pass rush (eighth in sack rate, seventh in pressure rate) remains solid, despite an ever-decreasing blitz rate (26.3%, 18th).

Detroit will be without top cornerback D.J. Reed for at least another week, but Brian Branch's return has allowed them to have a little more flexibility in their coverages, and it has calmed some things down (a little).

Player to watch: Hunter Henry. While Henry is far from 100% as he deals with a foot injury, he remains the biggest threat to Detroit's defense. Henry is first on the team in receptions (32) and second in yards (361). Meanwhile, the Lions is 31st in DVOA vs. tight ends.

Advantage: Patriots +3. This is the matchup that could change the game. New England, in my opinion, have one of the best passing attacks as long as they can avoid turnovers. The Lions aren't particularly great at forcing turnovers, creating just eight on the season—only three teams have fewer. So I would expect a pretty strong day from Maye.

New England hasn't run the ball very consistently, although injuries have played a large part in that, particularly as of late. They have a fullback, a running back, and three offensive linemen on IR. Running back TreVeyon Henderson has already been ruled out this week and top running back Rhamondre Stevenson could miss this one, too. Guard Ben Brown has been declared out already, and his backup Walter Rouse is working his way through concussion protocol. Put it all together, and the Patriots' running game ranks:

That said, running back Corey Kiner has stepped in and provided a bit of a spark in the last two weeks, rushing for 93 yards on just 15 carries, bolstered by a couple of 20-yard runs. Additionally, they are great in short-yardage situations, converting 75% of power situations, ranking fifth in the NFL.

The Lions' run defense is really starting to come on in recent weeks, with one of their best performances coming last week against a sneaky-good Dolphins run game. Overall, though, the numbers remain pretty pedestrian. The Lions rank:

They're still being victimized by some early-season explosives given up, but that has settled down considerably as of late.

What has been true pretty much all season is that Detroit has clogged the middle of the field. Led by the strong play of defensive tackles Tyleik Williams and Alim McNeill, anything between the b-gaps has been out of business. Yet, oddly, the Lions rank dead last in power success rate allowed, ceding an 84% conversion rate. So don't expect many third-and-1 stops from Detroit on Sunday.

Player to watch: Malcolm Rodriguez. Rodriguez is quietly having a very strong season, and with Jack Campbell out for the foreseeable future, his role will continue to increase. Currently, he sports an 83.9 PFF run defense grade (seventh in NFL among LBs) and the third-highest run stop percentage (11.7).

Advantage: Lions +1. With all of the injuries, I just can't trust the Patriots' rushing attack. And given that the Lions finally seem to have some answers on defense, this should favor the Lions. The one aspect that could tilt things in New England's favor, though, is Maye. While he still prefers to throw when he has escaped the pocket, he still does have 219 rushing yards this season, fourth most among quarterbacks.

While On Paper got wins both straight up and against the spread (6-2 overall, 4-4 ATS), it may have been my least accurate column of the year. I overestimated the Lions' advantage on offense and underestimated their advantages on defense. I expected a shootout and got a 19-9 win, instead. Sure, I had no idea Mahogany was going to be out, but that doesn't justify how bad I missed on that one.

To be fair, no one really expected a defensive battle. And the closest prediction in the comment section wasn't all that close: a 24-16 prediction from The Proudest:

I don't have photoshop on the road with me in Germany, so as your prize, enjoy this picture of me with a humongous beer in Munich:

The Lions come out with a slight +1 advantage, but the edge in three of the four matchups. As is the case with a lot of games this year, the outcome could come down to that one disadvantage: the Lions' pass defense. Maye is an electric player with a ton of explosive play potential. Detroit has done better at limiting that as of late, but the only recent quarterback they've played who can consistently do that was Jordan Love. If Detroit can repeat their performance from that game, they'll be in good shape. If not, it could be a long flight home.

Beond Airlines Suspends Flights Again: Will They Resume In December?

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Back in April 2026, we saw Beond Airlines suspend all scheduled operations for the summer, as the airline claimed it would resume flights as of October. Then in October, it announced it would return in December. Is that actually happening now, or what exactly is going on?

Beond Airlines launched operations in 2023, and it brands itself as the world’s first premium leisure airline. The company is headquartered in Dubai, but primarily operates flights to and from the Maldives. I reviewed the airline in 2024, and the onboard product was super pleasant. The airline currently has a fleet of two aircraft, including one A319 and one A321.

Anyway, in mid-April 2026, the company announced that it would suspend its scheduled operations for the entire summer. The airline promised to resume flights in October, for the winter season.

Then in October, the airline announced that it would suspend operations through November 30, 2026, meaning it will resume flights as of December 1, 2026. Interestingly, the airline has promised to provide a schedule update by November 12, 2026, but that date has now come and gone, and there was no update.

So I guess it remains to be seen if we actually see a resumption of flights as of December, or if the timeline will get pushed back again. The company does have a section on its travel updates page with FAQs, and one of the questions is “will the dates change again?” The answer is “we chose dates we are confident in,” but also notes that “if anything changes, you will hear from us first.”

Beond Airlines does have its “commitment” to customers when plans change, which includes the ability to change destinations or travel dates, receive a travel credit valid for 18 months, or to receive a full refund. A change of destinations or dates, plus a travel credit, isn’t worth a whole lot for an airline that keeps pushing back its relaunch.

Interestingly, but perhaps not surprisingly, earlier this year the airline deleted its customer promise webpage, which used to guarantee rebooking on another airline in business class in the event a flight is canceled, or else the airline would offer a free flight. That page is now gone, and there’s no mention of rebooking on another airline.

So the airline will “support you every step of the way,” unless you actually want to take the vacation you booked, in which case you’re out of luck (which is less than ideal, given that many Maldives properties have very strict cancelation policies).

While Beond Airlines’ business plan was questionable under the best of circumstances, the crisis of this year makes matters worse:

So I feel bad for Beond, and I’m not trying to do a “told you so, I was right” thing, because these are of course exceptional circumstances. The thing that made me suspicious about Beond for so long is that the airline would repeatedly make outlandish growth announcements, only to rarely follow through on them. That’s neither here nor there, though.

Keep in mind that since operations were initially suspended in April, we’ve seen some interesting developments:

My assumption is that Saudi Arabia is to some extent funding the Red Sea flights, given how the country is throwing money at any airline willing to fly there. However, one wonders if that’s enough money to make the math work, or if Beond even has the general funding right now to keep things operating.

It’s just all so strange, because it feels like half of the announcements from Beond Airlines are really targeting prospective investors, constantly promoting how it’s going to open new subsidiaries all over the world, launch dozens of new routes, etc.

As I said in April, I didn’t think it was a sure bet that flights would resume in October. Now we’ll see if flights actually resume in December.

In the spring of 2026, all-business class airline Beond suspended scheduled operations through October 2026. That timeline has come and gone, and now the airline claims it’ll resume operations as of December 2026. Over the summer, we learned that the airline wasn’t paying many staff for months, and the airline is now pivoting to operating service via Saudi Arabia’s Red Sea Airport.

We’ll see what happens in the coming weeks. Presumably the Red Sea service gets the airline some money from the Saudi government, but it remains to be seen if that’s enough to keep this operation afloat.

It’s understandable that the current situation is really challenging, between the price of jet fuel, plus overall sentiment about the Middle East. However, I have to imagine the airline didn’t have great financial health before this whole mess started, and suspending flights in some ways only makes things even worse.

How do you see this playing out for Beond?

“You Guys Are F***ing Idiots”: Jeff Kaplan Takes Aim At Blizzard’s Overwatch 2 Reveal

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Saying Overwatch 2 has had a rocky road since its 2019 reveal would be an understatement, and former Overwatch director Jeff Kaplan isn't a fan of how the game was first announced.

Speaking to Gaming World Interviews, Kaplan didn't mince words about the BlizzCon 2019 opening ceremony, where Overwatch 2 was shown for the first time, as Blizzard promised numerous features it either walked back or didn't deliver.

Kaplan said that from 2018 until his departure, he experienced constant friction with Blizzard's executive leadership over game direction, scheduling, and core game mechanics. This led him to confront leadership about the lack of a clear vision or business model for Overwatch 2, and to question the wisdom of announcing Overwatch 2 at the same time as Diablo 4.

"I got in a huge fight with, at the time, it wasn't Mike [Morhaime]. I loved Mike, who was the president of Blizzard, and another president of Blizzard, my executive producer, over announcing Overwatch 2. I went into their office, I’m like, 'What are we doing? We don’t really know what this game is; we don’t know what the business model is. This is a huge mistake.'"

According to Kaplan, the new executives "had a lot to prove" when they joined Blizzard. "They wanted to prove that 'Blizzard's back and Blizzard still has it.' And I'm like, 'You guys are f***ing idiots,' you know? I don’t know how else to put it, but after three years of just getting worn down and not agreeing with any of the decisions that were being made on the franchise and not feeling like I had agency anymore--like before it used to be like I'm the game director I get to decide the direction the game goes. And starting in 2018, I lost that, and after three years of fighting for it back, I realized this company belongs to these corporate people now, the Blizzard executive team, the Activision team."

Kaplan left Blizzard in 2021, saying Activision Blizzard’s financial arm told him that if Overwatch didn't meet certain financial goals, 1,000 people at the company would be laid off and the blame would fall on him. "The biggest f**k you moment I’ve had in my career--it felt surreal to be in that condition," Kaplan said earlier this year.

These days, Kaplan is working on The Legend of California at the new studio he co-founded, Kintsugiyama. The Blizzard veteran recently spoke to GameSpot about the "humbling" reality of being an indie dev and more.

Protein Startups Are Selling Lots Of Snacks And Drinks. Our Sample Tasting Found Most Neither Great Nor Horrible

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

In a perfect world, we would all eat meals and snacks that are convenient, taste great and provide optimal nutrition.

In reality, however, there are tradeoffs. We crave ice cream, chips and candy bars, which make carbs and fat easy to over-consume. Meeting protein goals, meanwhile, can require conscientious effort, particularly for those, like your author, who aren’t keen on eating lots of meat.

Fortunately, as we chronicled earlier this week, both startups and established food brands are making it simpler than ever to up protein intake. From sodas to snack bars to ice cream, it’s now possible to get a fix of this coveted macronutrient with foods and drinks more commonly associated with sugar, starch and fat.

The looming question is: Do they taste good? To find out, we hit up a local Target for a selection of protein foods sold by venture-backed startups. I and a 10-year-old then subjected them to a taste test.

The verdict? Most of this stuff is indeed edible and drinkable, albeit almost never nearly as tasty as the more protein-deficient alternatives, with just one exception.

No. 1: David Vanilla Bean Frozen Dessert: Anyone who buys or consumes this particular ice cream alternative will be doing so for the nutritional content, not the taste. A single pint packs in a whopping 30 grams of protein and has just 210 calories. The texture is also on point, creamy and easy to spoon, with a distinct vanilla flavor.

That said, you can’t hide the fact that this is not a product made with your typical ice cream ingredients. Those who don’t like artificial sweeteners or the flavor and texture of whey protein will want to pass. For myself and my co-taster, however, it was a reasonably appealing eating experience. Not as good as traditional ice cream, but not bad for a high-protein food.

Made by: Medici Brands (David parent company).

No. 2 SkyPop Protein Soda: The nutritional stats for SkyPop Protein Soda are also remarkable. A 12-ounce can has just 45 calories, with 10 grams of protein and a single gram of carbohydrates. It’s also properly fizzy, and makes a satisfying pop when you open the can.

But what about flavor? Our local store only had the Ice Pop flavor in stock and it was, in both tasters’ view, not horrible but not something we’d plan to buy regularly. I could see consuming it when on a clear fluids diet, as is the case before some medical procedures, as it’d be one of the few available sources of protein.

No. 3: Khloud Protein Popcorn: Unlike many of our other featured foods, Khloud Protein Popcorn isn’t a super protein-rich offering. A serving contains 7 grams of protein and 160 calories, most of which come from carbs and fat. Still, its protein count is higher than other packaged popcorn, which typically has maybe 2 protein grams in a comparable serving.

Overall, the tasters’ conclusion, from our trial of White Cheddar Khloud, was that this is a decent snack food. The flavor of milk protein and whey protein concentrate was palpable, but not overwhelming. It’s not exactly a snack food to savor, but for something to casually munch at one’s desk or while watching a show, it served its purpose.

No. 4 Mosh: In the third season of “The White Lotus”, Mosh co-founder Patrick Schwarzenegger plays a fitness and finance bro who makes a daily high-protein shake that other family members find revolting. Perhaps this was inspired by Mosh, the brain-supporting protein bar startup he launched with his mother, Maria Shriver.

The Mosh bar, which we purchased in the peanut butter cup flavor, crams 20 grams of protein, 6 grams of fiber, plus brain-supporting ingredients like creatine and lion’s mane, into a compact, shelf-stable bar. It’s an impressive feat. Yet while our brains’ may have appreciated the nutrition and supplement boost the Mosh bar provided, our taste buds decidedly did not.

I’d put Mosh in the functional food category. It’s something that you might eat if you wanted that particular nutrient mix. But it’s not something to consume for pleasure.

No. 5 Cadootz Crackers: We tried two varieties of Cadootz Crackers — cheddar and sea salt — and walked away neither enthused nor repulsed.

For people interested in a gluten- and seed oil-free small snack cracker with about 5 grams of protein per serving, Cadootz rises to the occasion. It’s got a nice crunch and decent flavor.

As someone who’s not particularly seeking to avoid gluten and seed oils or to get a modest protein bump from my crackers, I’ll probably pass on follow-on purchases. But for its target audience, Cadootz should serve well.

No. 6 Frozen One: For those who thought the David frozen dessert was a marvel of protein engineering, the folks at Frozen One would like a word. Their protein ice cream has a whopping 40 grams of protein in each pint.

Yes, but how does it taste? Not that bad, according to our two-person tasting team. We tried both the chocolate and apple pie varieties, preferring the former. Like the David offering, Frozen One tastes like a product made with milk protein concentrate and sugar substitutes. You won’t mistake it for Ben and Jerry’s. But if the goal is to consume a steak-like quantity of protein in the form factor of a pint of ice cream, it does its job.

No. 7 Brami Protein Pasta: We saved the best for last. That, unexpectedly, was Brami Protein Pasta, a noodle made from semolina and lupini beans that boasts about 70% more protein than standard pasta and more than double the fiber.

With about 12 grams of protein for a 200 calorie serving, Brami isn’t a dense protein source. But, with the right topping, it’s enough to transform a typically starchy noodle meal into something reasonably protein rich.

Most notably, however, Brami tastes quite good, with no discernable weird aftertaste or texture issues. I made both standard and Brami noodles to taste the difference. For seconds, I went with the Brami.

How Agents Can Chart a Course for No Buyer Remorse

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The bidding war is over. Some of the decisions made are harder to undo. As an agent or broker, is it part of your professional obligation to discuss where the buyer might be in five years financially and family-wise? Probably, even if it might occasionally put them off a purchase.

A recent survey of 1,000 U.S. property owners found that 80% had at least one regret about their home, location or buying experience. Location bothered 45%, while 58% reported regrets about the home or homeownership itself.

For homeowners who bought during the pandemic frenzy, moving on can carry an added complication: the mortgage may be more attractive than the house.

A recent study in the Journal of Banking & Finance found that for every percentage-point increase between a homeowner’s fixed mortgage rate and current market rates, the probability of selling falls 18.1%. Researchers estimate mortgage lock-in was associated with 1.72 million fewer home sales between Q2 2022 and Q2 2024.

That pressure is particularly relevant now. The average 30-year mortgage rate reached 7.28% in early October, its highest level in nearly three years.

For Nick Nastos, a real estate broker and founder of Chicago’s Property Shop, that creates an unusual form of buyer’s remorse.

“You can regret the house and still have a mortgage you’d hate to give up,” he says. “That’s what makes these decisions difficult. Someone may know the home no longer fits their life, but replacing a 3% mortgage with today’s rate can completely change the math.”

Nastos gives five reasons a pandemic purchase may feel different now.

Nastos says regret alone should not determine whether someone sells.

“Start with what you would change about the house, then price out what changing homes would actually cost,” he says. “Sometimes the answer is to move. Sometimes that low mortgage makes improving the home you already own the better option.”

Suzy Minken, a real estate advisor with Compass working out of Northern Virginia, said looking toward the future while purchasing a house today is key to avoiding buyer remorse.

“It’s not something that is talked about enough,” she says. “I’ve even had buyers speak to my architect (relationships are key) while touring the house to see if they could do an addition. The architect did a review of the property and said it could be done. The buyers submitted an offer (multiple bid situation) and bought the house. They proceeded with a two-story addition before they moved into the house.”

Minken stresses that there is a need to distinguish between buyer’s remorse and the frustration of having fewer options than originally anticipated.

“Consider the young family who purchased a starter home with the intention of moving to a larger home in five years,” she explains. “Perhaps they’ve welcomed another child and need an additional bedroom. But with higher home prices and mortgage rates, the move-up home they envisioned may no longer be financially practical.

“In many ways, today’s move-up buyer has become today’s renovation buyer. Rather than moving to a larger home, homeowners are looking for ways to make their existing homes accommodate their changing needs.”

But what happens when renovation isn’t an option either?

“When buyers fall in love with a home, I encourage them to think about whether it’s already ‘maxed out’ or has room to grow with their changing needs,” says Minken. “Is there room to expand? Could an unfinished basement become additional living space? Is there flexibility in the floor plan to accommodate a growing family?

“I also encourage buyers to look beyond the house itself. Why are the current owners selling? Did they outgrow the home after having another child? Is there something about the layout or the property that made adapting it difficult? These questions can reveal important clues about how a home might serve its next owners.”

Minken encourages buyers to think beyond what they need today and consider how their needs might change over time. That includes family circumstances, the need for additional space and even commuting considerations if their employer requires them to spend more time in the office.

“We can’t predict changes in interest rates, home prices or family circumstances,” she says. “But we can ask the right questions before they make one of the largest financial decisions of their lives. Sometimes the regret isn’t buying the wrong home. It’s discovering that the next step, whether moving to a bigger house or renovating, is no longer within reach.”

Michael Catarevas is a senior editor for RISMedia.

Routine maintenance, sensible storage, suitable flood protection and a clear emergency plan can help homeowners prepare more effectively. The right approach depends on the property, its surroundings and the conditions it may face. Read more.

In a policy switch, the Fed says its October 2028 meeting will not see a vote on interest rates

📅 2026-11-12  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The Federal Reserve will use its October meeting in 2027 as a nonvoting session, a departure that is part of a broader initiative from Chairman Kevin Warsh aimed at reducing the central bank's market footprint.

According to an announcement Thursday, the rate-setting Federal Open Market Committee will set aside its Oct. 26-27 session for "the discussion of broad monetary policy strategy issues."

Warsh announced the move at his October news conference, though he did not set a date.

In 2027, there will still be seven voting meetings, with the October session set up as extra time for policy deliberation without a formal vote or post-meeting statement.

"Now, what's the reasoning behind this change? To me, it's quite straightforward," the chairman said in explaining his rationale. "Getting policy right involves assessing near-term developments at timely, regular, and information-rich intervals."

"But it's equally important we reserve time and space, as an intellectually diverse group, for strategic deliberations—for thinking anew about big questions, looking out over longer time horizons, focusing, as I say, on the left side of the decimal point," he said.

Warsh has been critical of the Fed's prior communications strategy, saying policymakers spent too much time providing guidance on policy moves that handcuffed them when it came time to make decisions.
